<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that, for every fixed $`0\lt \delta\lt 1/3`$, it is NP-hard to distinguish three-colorable graphs from graphs in which every independent set has fewer than <i>δ</i> times the number of vertices. Consequently, for every fixed integer <i>c</i> ≥ 3, finding a proper <i>c</i>-coloring of a three-colorable graph is NP-hard.

- **claim-coloring-independence-gap** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every fixed real $0<\delta<1/3$, there is a deterministic polynomial-time algorithm $R_\delta$ that maps each explicitly encoded Boolean $3$-CNF formula $\phi$ to a nonempty finite simple undirected unweighted graph $G_\phi$ such that $$\begin{array}{ll} \phi\text{ is satisfiable} &\Longrightarrow G_\phi\text{ is three-colorable},\\[2pt] \phi\text{ is unsatisfiable} &\Longrightarrow \alpha(G_\phi)<\delta\,\|V(G_\phi)\|. \end{array}$$ The running time and explicit output size are polynomial in the ordinary binary encoding length of $\phi$. Their constants and polynomial degree may depend on the fixed $\delta$, but not on $\phi$. The coloring in the first implication covers every vertex and is not supplied with the graph.
  - Explanation: A deterministic reduction preserves satisfiability as three-colorability of the entire output graph and converts unsatisfiability into a strict independent-set density bound. Its polynomial guarantees hold with the density threshold fixed.
- **claim-fixed-palette-hardness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-coloring)): For every fixed pair of integers $3\le k\le c$, it is NP-hard to distinguish $k$-colorable graphs from graphs that are not $c$-colorable.
  - Explanation: The independent-set gap implies hardness of distinguishing the promised smaller palette from failure of any fixed larger palette.
- **claim-perfect-completeness-label-cover** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-thm-label-cover)): For every fixed rational $\sigma\in(0,1)$ there are nonempty constant alphabets and a deterministic polynomial-time reduction from $3$SAT to explicit Label Cover instances with nonempty occurrence lists such that satisfiable formulas have value $1$ and unsatisfiable formulas have value at most $\sigma$.
  - Explanation: The standard deterministic constraint reduction supplies perfect completeness and arbitrarily small fixed soundness over constant nonempty alphabets.
- **claim-dimension-independent-junta** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-junta)): For every $0\le L<\infty$ and $\tau>0$ there is an integer $J(L,\tau)\ge1$ such that every $L$-Lipschitz function $f:[0,1]^N\to[-1,1]$, for every positive integer $N$, admits a junta on at most $J(L,\tau)$ coordinates with $L^2$ error less than $\tau$. The junta may be taken to be a coordinate conditional expectation of $f$.
  - Explanation: A Lipschitz function admits a coordinate conditional expectation approximation whose coordinate budget is independent of the ambient dimension.
- **claim-dimension-independent-influences** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-influences)): Fix $0\le L<\infty$ and $\varepsilon>0$. There are $\beta>0$ and an integer $K\ge1$, independent of $s$, such that every $L$-Lipschitz $H:\mathbb T^s\to[-1,1]$ satisfies:* 1. *if $\mathbb EH=0$ and $\mathbb EH^2>\varepsilon$, then $D_i(H)\ge\beta$ for some $i$;* 2. *at most $K$ indices satisfy $D_i(H)\ge\beta/2$.
  - Explanation: Substantial mean-zero variance forces a substantial coordinate influence, while only boundedly many coordinates can exceed the smaller influence threshold.
- **claim-exact-finite-encoding** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-prop-encoding)): For fixed choices of the parameters and Label Cover alphabets, this rule constructs a nonempty finite simple unweighted graph in deterministic polynomial time and output size, measured in the size of the explicit Label Cover instance. Composing with Theorem <a href="#thm:label-cover" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:label-cover">3</a> for any fixed $\sigma$ gives polynomial time and output size in the binary encoding length of the original $3$-CNF formula.
  - Explanation: Exact shortest paths and rational multiplicity expansion produce an explicit simple unweighted graph deterministically. Polynomial size and time are measured with the construction parameters and alphabets fixed.
- **claim-all-vertex-completeness** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-completeness)): If the Label Cover instance has value $1$, the clique branch does not occur and the constructed graph has a proper three-coloring.
  - Explanation: A satisfying constraint labeling gives a nonexpanding circle evaluation, excludes the clique branch, and yields a proper coloring of every multiplicity copy.
- **claim-bounded-subchain-lists** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-subchain-lists)): For every fixed occurrence chain and every nonempty $I\subseteq[r]$ with $\|I\|\le s$, there are sets $S_I^j\subseteq M_j$, $j\in I$, with $$\label{eq:list-size} \sum_{j\in I}\|S_I^j\|\le d, \qquad d:=\max\{1,J(sL,\gamma)(m+1)^s\},$$ such that, for every $t\in\mathcal T_m^I$, a junta $g_{I,t}$ using only scalar coordinates $(j,\ell)$ with $\ell\in S_I^j$ satisfies $$\label{eq:subchain-junta} \\|h_{I,t}-g_{I,t}\\|_2<\gamma.$$ The lists can be chosen as functions only of the subchain’s label sets, internal projection maps, and functions $F_j$, $j\in I$. Identical subchain data receive identical choices.
  - Explanation: A bounded union of scalar coordinates approximates every allowed coefficient choice on a small subchain. The lists depend only on that subchain's data and are used solely in the soundness analysis.
- **claim-separated-list-collision** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-separated-lists)): For every fixed $I,J$ with $1\le\|I\|,\|J\|\le s$ and $\max I<\min J$, $$\Pr_{\text{chain}}(A_I\cap A_J\ne\varnothing)\le d^2\sigma.$$
  - Explanation: Conditioning away the separating occurrence makes each candidate list depend only on its own question. A shared terminal answer then yields a successful original constraint strategy, so a union bound controls collisions.
- **claim-distributional-alignment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-alignment)): For every pair of integers $s,d\geq 1$ and every real $\eta>0$, there exist an integer $r\geq s$ and a rational probability distribution $\mu$ on $\binom{[r]}s$ with the following property.Let $M_*$ be any set, and let $A_I\subseteq M_*$, $\|A_I\|\leq d$, be specified for every nonempty $I\subseteq[r]$ with $\|I\|\leq s$. Assume that $$\label{align:separation} A_I\cap A_J=\varnothing \qquad\text{whenever }\max I<\min J.$$ Then, with probability at least $1-\eta$ over $B\sim\mu$, there exists a map $a:M_*\to B$ such that $$\label{align:conclusion} a(A_I)\subseteq I \qquad\text{for every nonempty }I\subseteq B.$$ The map $a$ may depend on the list family and on $B$.
  - Explanation: A rational sampling law chosen before the lists makes common-index assignments likely for every bounded separated list family, independently of its label universe.
- **claim-high-probability-aligned-pairs** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-good-pairs)): Under these choices, for independent uniform chain sampling and $B\sim\mu$, the probability that the pair is not aligned is at most $4^r d^2\sigma+\varepsilon$.
  - Explanation: The probability of an unaligned chain and layer set is bounded by separated-list collisions plus the failure allowance of distributional alignment.
- **claim-aligned-conditional-density** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-aligned-bound)): Assume <a href="#eq:parameter-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:parameter-bounds">[eq:parameter-bounds]</a>. Suppose that the construction does not take the clique branch, and let $\mathcal A$ be a nonempty independent set of its output graph. Form the functions and lists associated with $\mathcal A$ as above. For every chain and $s$-element set $B$ admitting a map $$a:M_r\longrightarrow B, \qquad a(A_I)\subseteq I \quad\text{for every nonempty }I\subseteq B,$$ the conditional probability that the sampled output vertex belongs to $\mathcal A$ is at most $6\varepsilon$.
  - Explanation: For a fixed aligned chain and layer set, influence detection, geometric coefficient weights, phase absorption, and exact grid coupling bound the conditional independent-set density.

        "exact_quote": "For every pair of integers $s,d\\geq 1$ and every real $\\eta>0$, there exist an integer $r\\geq s$ and a rational probability distribution $\\mu$ on $\\binom{[r]}s$ with the following property.Let $M_*$ be any set, and let $A_I\\subseteq M_*$, $|A_I|\\leq d$, be specified for every nonempty $I\\subseteq[r]$ with $|I|\\leq s$. For every chain and $s$-element set $B$ admitting a map $$a:M_r\\longrightarrow B,\n \\qquad a(A_I)\\subseteq I\n \\quad\\text{for every nonempty }I\\subseteq B,$$ the conditional probability that the sampled output vertex belongs to $\\mathcal A$ is at most $6\\varepsilon$.",
      "explanation": "For a fixed aligned chain and layer set, influence detection, geometric coefficient weights, phase absorption, and exact grid coupling bound the conditional independent-set density.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-aligned-bound",
          "exact_quote": "Assume <a href=\"#eq:parameter-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:parameter-bounds\">[eq:parameter-bounds]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-106.1"
        },
        {

          "exact_quote": "Suppose that the construction does not take the clique branch, and let $\\mathcal A$ be a nonempty independent set of its output graph.",
          "source_id": "PM-106.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "eq:parameter-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "Keep the tolerance $\\varepsilon>0$ of Section <a href=\"#sec:alignment\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:alignment\">5</a>, and take $\\beta>0$ and $K\\ge1$ from Lemma <a href=\"#lem:influences\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:influences\">5</a> with $L=64$ and this $\\varepsilon$. For the graph parameters of Section <a href=\"#sec:construction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:construction\">3</a> and the list-approximation tolerance $\\gamma$ of Section <a href=\"#sec:lists\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:lists\">4</a>, assume $$\\label{eq:parameter-bounds}\n \\begin{gathered}\n L/m<\\beta/2,\\qquad \\lambda K<\\varepsilon,\n \\qquad (1-p_m)^s<\\varepsilon,\\\\\n \\frac{s(2\\gamma)^2}{\\beta^2}<\\varepsilon,\\qquad\n \\frac{2L}{D}<\\gamma,\\qquad \\frac{2Ls}{P}<\\varepsilon.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Section <a href=\"#sec:parameters\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:parameters\">7</a> will choose constants satisfying these inequalities. A coefficient equal to $1$ is called a *full coefficient*. Although its individual probability $p_m$ may be small, the third inequality makes at least one of the $s$ coefficients full with probability greater than $1-\\varepsilon$.",

        }

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Proper graph coloring** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A proper coloring of a graph assigns different colors to adjacent vertices.
- **Independent set** ([source](#pm-h-2)): An independent set is a set of pairwise nonadjacent vertices.
- **Vertex count and independence number** ([source](#pm-h-2)): For a nonempty finite graph $G$, write $n=\|V(G)\|$ and let $\alpha(G)$ be its maximum independent-set size.
- **Label Cover instance** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A *Label Cover instance* consists of finite question sets $U,V$, nonempty finite answer alphabets $\Sigma_L,\Sigma_R$, and a nonempty explicit list $\mathcal C$ of test occurrences. An occurrence $c$ has questions $u_c\in U$, $v_c\in V$, and a total map $\pi_c:\Sigma_L\to\Sigma_R$.
- **Label Cover value** ([source](#pm-h-6)): The value is $$\mathop{\mathrm{val}}(\mathcal C)= \max_{\ell:U\to\Sigma_L,\ \rho:V\to\Sigma_R} \Pr_{c\in\mathcal C}\bigl[\pi_c(\ell(u_c))=\rho(v_c)\bigr],$$ where occurrences, including repeated occurrences, are sampled uniformly.
- **Coordinate junta** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A function is a *junta on $S$* if it depends only on the input coordinates in $S$.
- **Sup metric convention** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): All products of metric spaces carry the sup metric unless stated otherwise.
- **Circle distance** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): The circle $\mathbb T=\mathbb R/\mathbb Z$ carries the distance $d_{\mathbb T}(x,y)=\min_{k\in\mathbb Z}\|x-y-k\|$.
- **Coefficient law** ([source](#eq:coefficient-law)): The coefficient values and their probabilities are $$\label{eq:coefficient-law} \mathcal T_m=\{0,1/m,\ldots,1\},\qquad p_k=\Pr(t=k/m)=\frac{\lambda^k}{\sum_{\ell=0}^m\lambda^\ell} \quad(0\le k\le m).$$
- **Extended shortest-path pseudometric** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Let $\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}$ be the resulting shortest-path extended pseudometric, with distance infinity between components.
- **Half-shift involution** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Let $T$ add $1/2$ to every phase coordinate, within each copy.
- **Aligned chain and layer set** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-1)): A pair consisting of a chain and a set $B\in\binom{[r]}s$ is *aligned* if there is a map $a:M_r\to B$ such that $a(A_I)\subseteq I$ for every nonempty $I\subseteq B$.
- **Full coefficient** ([source](#eq:parameter-bounds)): A coefficient equal to $1$ is called a *full coefficient*.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-unsatisfiable-independence-density | \alpha(G_\phi)/\|V(G_\phi)\| | < | $\delta$ | The density threshold is fixed before the input formula. |
| q-fallback-clique-size | q | > | $1/\delta$ | A fixed integer chosen before invoking the constraint reduction. |
| q-antipodal-edge-threshold | Output edge distance threshold | = | $1/8$ | The same fixed threshold is used for every pair of distinct output vertices. |
| q-uniform-analytic-lipschitz | L | = | $64$ | Independent of all tuple dimensions and alphabet sizes. |
| q-subchain-list-capacity | \sum_{j\in I}\|S_I^j\| | ≤ | $d$ | The list capacity is independent of the Label Cover alphabets. |
| q-subchain-list-capacity-formula | d | = | $\max\{1,J(sL,\gamma)(m+1)^s\}$ | Fixed before the layer count, source soundness, and answer alphabets. |
| q-separated-list-intersection | \Pr_{\text{chain}}(A_I\cap A_J\ne\varnothing) | ≤ | $d^2\sigma$ | The layer sets are fixed before the separating occurrence is sampled. |
| q-unaligned-pair-probability | Probability that a sampled chain and layer set are not aligned | ≤ | $4^r d^2\sigma+\varepsilon$ | The sampling law is selected before the lists arising from an independent set. |
| q-aligned-conditional-membership | Conditional probability that the sampled vertex belongs to the independent set | ≤ | $6\varepsilon$ | All coefficient, approximation, and discretization inequalities are assumed. |
| q-explicit-vertex-count | Total output vertex count outside the clique branch | = | $C_0N^{r-1}$ | The common conditional denominator and layer count are fixed; the occurrence-list size varies. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-perfect-completeness-source** [claim](#stmt-thm-label-cover) / [proof](#pm-h-6): Apply the standard perfect-completeness constraint reduction with fixed repetition chosen for the required soundness. -> An explicit occurrence-list Label Cover instance with perfect completeness and bounded soundness.
- **step-continuous-junta-input** [claim](#stmt-thm-junta) / [proof](#pm-h-7): Use the continuous junta theorem and bounded range to obtain a coordinate conditional expectation approximation. -> A dimension-independent coordinate budget.
- **step-influence-detection-and-count** [claim](#stmt-lem-influences) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-influences): Combine coordinate conditional expectations with orthogonal decomposition; apply a second junta approximation to exclude influences outside its coordinates. -> A variance-detection threshold and a dimension-independent influence count.
- **step-exact-graph-encoding** [claim](#stmt-prop-encoding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-encoding): Enumerate tuple grids and projection links, compute exact integer shortest paths, test the clique branch, and expand rational outcome probabilities into vertex multiplicities. -> A nonempty explicit finite simple unweighted graph in deterministic polynomial time.
- **step-satisfying-circle-evaluation** [claim](#stmt-lem-completeness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-completeness): Evaluate each phase vector at its satisfying product answer; use nonexpansion across all links and partition circle phases into the prescribed half-open color intervals. -> Exclusion of the clique branch and a proper coloring of every output vertex.
- **step-independent-set-functions** [claim](#stmt-lem-subchain-lists) / [proof](#pm-h-12): Build odd distance bumps from independent-set locations, extend within tuple tori, clip, and antisymmetrize. -> A fixed family of odd bounded Lipschitz functions with uniform chain compatibility.
- **step-subchain-coordinate-unions** [claim](#stmt-lem-subchain-lists) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-subchain-lists): Approximate every coefficient vector by a coordinate conditional expectation and unite the selected coordinates within each block using a fixed ordering rule. -> Alphabet-independent bounded lists that are local to their subchains.
- **step-separated-list-decoding** [claim](#stmt-lem-separated-lists) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-separated-lists): Condition on all occurrences except the separating test, extract own-question candidate answers, and union-bound their deterministic strategies. -> A collision probability bound for every fixed separated pair of layer sets.
- **step-alignment-minimax** [claim](#stmt-lem-alignment) / [proof](#pm-h-23): Replace labels by finite equality patterns and apply finite minimax to the assumed failure of a universal sampling law. -> A hypothetical random pattern with positive bad-set probability on every selected layer set.
- **step-alignment-order-invariant-limit** [claim](#stmt-lem-alignment) / [proof](#pm-h-24): Ramsey-homogenize discretized finite probability vectors, pass to consistent limiting laws, and construct the countable order-invariant array. -> An order-invariant rational-indexed list pattern retaining positive bad-set probability.
- **step-alignment-invariant-cut** [claim](#stmt-lem-alignment) / [proof](#pm-h-25): Associate each occupied label class with the supremum of its occurrence minima; invariance under order automorphisms forces that cut into every occurrence set. -> A common occurrence index for every label class, contradicting badness.
- **step-rational-alignment-law** [claim](#stmt-lem-alignment) / [proof](#pm-h-26): Approximate the real sampling law inside its finite simplex by a rational law while preserving the required failure margin. -> A finite rational sampling law independent of the label universe.
- **step-aligned-pair-probability** [claim](#stmt-lem-good-pairs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-good-pairs): Union-bound all separated-list collision events and then apply the universal alignment law on each collision-free chain. -> A bound on the probability of an unaligned chain and layer set.
- **step-aligned-coefficient-density** [claim](#stmt-prop-aligned-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-aligned-bound): Introduce auxiliary phases, control zero-coefficient influences using aligned juntas, control positive coefficients by geometric level decrease, then absorb phases in a full block and couple to grid rotations. -> The conditional independent-set membership bound.
- **step-parameter-order-and-main-gap** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-aligned-bound-2): Choose analytic and finite construction constants before the source alphabets, then average the aligned conditional bound and unaligned failure allowance using the exact uniform vertex law. -> The deterministic satisfiability-to-coloring and independent-set gap reduction.
- **step-fixed-palette-consequence** [claim](#stmt-cor-coloring) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-coloring): Choose the density threshold below the independent-set density forced by the larger fixed palette. -> NP-hardness of the fixed-palette coloring distinction.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-exact-multiplicity**: Expanding rational outcome probabilities into integer multiplicities makes uniform output-vertex sampling reproduce the supplied finite experiment exactly.
- **inv-antipodal-edge**: Distinct output vertices are adjacent exactly when the location of one is within the prescribed pseudometric threshold of the half-shift of the other.
- **inv-metric-clique-branch**: The construction emits the prescribed clique when an auxiliary point is sufficiently close to its own half-shift.
- **inv-exact-disconnected-distances**: Shortest-path comparisons use exact scaled integer lengths and preserve infinity between disconnected components.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-uniform-density-parameter**: Treat the fixed-threshold polynomial reduction as a uniform efficient procedure for arbitrarily changing the density threshold. -> The finite encoding procedure does not compute the analytic constants required for all thresholds.
- **anti-full-hardness-certificate**: Interpret successful finite encoding tests as a certificate of the complete independence-density hardness theorem. -> The tests establish neither the Label Cover reduction nor the continuous analytic soundness argument.
- **anti-discard-multiplicity**: Merge output vertices that share a location or give every elementary outcome the same multiplicity. -> Uniform sampling no longer reproduces the rational experiment, so the passage from conditional probabilities to vertex density is unsupported.
- **anti-output-coloring**: Use the encoding routine to recover a satisfying labeling or a proper coloring automatically. -> The graph construction provides no satisfying-labeling search procedure.
- **anti-rounded-threshold**: Replace exact threshold comparisons with floating-point approximations or discard the clique test. -> Boundary edges can change, and zero-link identifications can make a point close to its own antipode.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Proposition 6 gives a finite exact encoding procedure. This executable specialization reconstructs the auxiliary geometry from explicit phase spaces and total projection maps, then expands an already supplied rational outcome experiment. It certifies the encoding kernel rather than the complete formula reduction. Source: @@proof-stmt-prop-encoding@@After multiplying lengths by $D$,@@with infinity stored separately.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON with D, q, spaces, maps, and outcomes.
# PRECONDITIONS: D is positive and even; q is positive; spaces have nonempty answer sets; maps are total projections; nonnegative rational outcome weights sum to one.
# OUTPUTS: JSON containing branch, n, and the sorted edges of the explicit simple unweighted graph.
# TIME: Polynomial in the enumerated auxiliary graph and expanded outcome list; enumeration itself depends on the supplied grid dimensions.
# SPACE: Quadratic in the enumerated auxiliary graph, plus the expanded output graph.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integers and rational numbers; disconnected distances remain None.
# FAILURE: Invalid finite data raise an exception and produce no certificate.
import sys, json, itertools, math
from fractions import Fraction

def require(ok):
    if not ok:
        raise ValueError('invalid finite input')

def main(a):
    D, q = a['D'], a['q']
    require(type(D) is int and D > 0 and D % 2 == 0)
    require(type(q) is int and q > 0)
    spaces = a['spaces']
    require(isinstance(spaces, list) and len(spaces) > 0)
    require(all(type(k) is int and k > 0 for k in spaces))
    nodes, index, blocks = [], {}, []
    for sid, k in enumerate(spaces):
        block = []
        for x in itertools.product(range(D), repeat=k):
            idx = len(nodes)
            nodes.append((sid, x))
            index[(sid, x)] = idx
            block.append(idx)
        blocks.append(block)
    N = len(nodes)
    dist = [[None] * N for _ in range(N)]
    for i in range(N):
        dist[i][i] = 0
    for block in blocks:
        for i in block:
            x = nodes[i][1]
            for j in block:
                y = nodes[j][1]
                dist[i][j] = max(min(abs(u-v), D-abs(u-v)) for u,v in zip(x,y))
    for p in a['maps']:
        src, dst, projection = p['source'], p['target'], p['projection']
        require(type(src) is int and type(dst) is int)
        require(0 <= src < len(spaces) and 0 <= dst < len(spaces))
        require(isinstance(projection, list) and len(projection) == spaces[src])
        require(all(type(h) is int and 0 <= h < spaces[dst] for h in projection))
        for j in blocks[dst]:
            y = nodes[j][1]
            i = index[(src, tuple(y[h] for h in projection))]
            dist[i][j] = dist[j][i] = 0
    for k in range(N):
        for i in range(N):
            if dist[i][k] is None:
                continue
            for j in range(N):
                if dist[k][j] is None:
                    continue
                v = dist[i][k] + dist[k][j]
                if dist[i][j] is None or v < dist[i][j]:
                    dist[i][j] = v
    shifted = []
    for sid, x in nodes:
        shifted.append(index[(sid, tuple((u + D // 2) % D for u in x))])
    def near(i, j):
        v = dist[i][j]
        return v is not None and 8 * v <= D
    raw = []
    for outcome in a['outcomes']:
        sid, x = outcome['space'], outcome['point']
        require(type(sid) is int and 0 <= sid < len(spaces))
        require(isinstance(x, list) and len(x) == spaces[sid])
        require(all(type(u) is int and 0 <= u < D for u in x))
        w = Fraction(outcome['weight'])
        require(w >= 0)
        raw.append((index[(sid, tuple(x))], w))
    require(sum((w for _, w in raw), Fraction(0)) == 1)
    if any(near(i, shifted[i]) for i in range(N)):
        return {'branch':'clique', 'n':q, 'edges':[[i,j] for i in range(q) for j in range(i+1,q)]}
    denominator = 1
    for _, w in raw:
        denominator = math.lcm(denominator, w.denominator)
    locations = []
    for location, w in raw:
        copies = w.numerator * (denominator // w.denominator)
        locations.extend([location] * copies)
    edges = []
    for i in range(len(locations)):
        for j in range(i+1, len(locations)):
            if near(locations[i], shifted[locations[j]]):
                edges.append([i,j])
    return {'branch':'metric', 'n':len(locations), 'edges':edges}

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(json.dumps(main(json.load(sys.stdin)), sort_keys=True, separators=(',',':')))
```

D // 2) % D for u in x)\n    def passes(source, target):\n        length = distances[source].get(target)\n        return length is not None and Fraction(length, D) <= Fraction(1,8)\n    if any(passes(node, antipode(node)) for node in graph):\n        q = a['q']\n        return {'branch':'clique','n':q,'edges':[list(pair) for pair in itertools.combinations(range(q),2)]}\n    probabilities = [Fraction(o['weight']) for o in a['outcomes']]\n    denominator = math.lcm(*(p.denominator for p in probabilities))\n    vertices = []\n    for outcome, probability in zip(a['outcomes'], probabilities):\n        count = probability * denominator\n        if count.denominator != 1:\n            raise ValueError('nonintegral expansion')\n        vertices += [(outcome['space'], tuple(outcome['point']))] * int(count)\n    edge_set = []\n    for i,j in itertools.combinations(range(len(vertices)),2):\n        if passes(vertices[i], antipode(vertices[j])):\n            edge_set.append([i,j])\n    return {'branch':'metric','n':len(vertices),'edges':edge_set}\n\ndef main():\n    with open(sys.argv[1]) as handle:\n        instance = json.load(handle)\n    with open(sys.argv[2]) as handle:\n        candidate = json.load(handle)\n    expected = json.loads(sys.argv[3])\n    actual = derive(instance)\n    if not isinstance(candidate, dict) or set(candidate) != {'branch','n','edges'}:\n        return 1\n    if type(candidate['n']) is not int or not isinstance(candidate['branch'], str):\n        return 1\n    if not isinstance(candidate['edges'], list):\n        return 1\n    for edge in candidate['edges']:\n        if not isinstance(edge,list) or len(edge) != 2 or any(type(v) is not int for v in edge):\n            return 1\n    return 0 if candidate == actual and expected == actual else 1\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    try:\n        sys.exit(main())\n    except Exception:\n        sys.exit(1)\n",
    "output_contract": "Return JSON with branch equal to metric or clique, n equal to the number of distinct emitted vertices, and edges as lexicographically ordered pairs of vertex indices. Otherwise use the least common denominator of the supplied reduced rational weights to expand outcomes and apply the same non-strict threshold to each distinct vertex pair. Zero-weight outcomes emit no vertices. Disconnected auxiliary points never pass a distance threshold.",

import Fraction\n\ndef require(ok):\n    if not ok:\n        raise ValueError('invalid finite input')\n\ndef main(a):\n    D, q = a['D'], a['q']\n    require(type(D) is int and D > 0 and D % 2 == 0)\n    require(type(q) is int and q > 0)\n    spaces = a['spaces']\n    require(isinstance(spaces, list) and len(spaces) > 0)\n    require(all(type(k) is int and k > 0 for k in spaces))\n    nodes, index, blocks = [], {}, []\n    for sid, k in enumerate(spaces):\n        block = []\n        for x in itertools.product(range(D), repeat=k):\n            idx = len(nodes)\n            nodes.append((sid, x))\n            index[(sid, x)] = idx\n            block.append(idx)\n        blocks.append(block)\n    N = len(nodes)\n    dist = [[None] * N for _ in range(N)]\n    for i in range(N):\n        dist[i][i] = 0\n    for block in blocks:\n        for i in block:\n            x = nodes[i][1]\n            for j in block:\n                y = nodes[j][1]\n                dist[i][j] = max(min(abs(u-v), D-abs(u-v)) for u,v in zip(x,y))\n    for p in a['maps']:\n        src, dst, projection = p['source'], p['target'], p['projection']\n        require(type(src) is int and type(dst) is int)\n        require(0 <= src < len(spaces) and 0 <= dst < len(spaces))\n        require(isinstance(projection, list) and len(projection) == spaces[src])\n        require(all(type(h) is int and 0 <= h < spaces[dst] for h in projection))\n        for j in blocks[dst]:\n            y = nodes[j][1]\n            i = index[(src, tuple(y[h] for h in projection))]\n            dist[i][j] = dist[j][i] = 0\n    for k in range(N):\n        for i in range(N):\n            if dist[i][k] is None:\n                continue\n            for j in range(N):\n                if dist[k][j] is None:\n                    continue\n                v = dist[i][k] + dist[k][j]\n                if dist[i][j] is None or v < dist[i][j]:\n                    dist[i][j] = v\n    shifted = []\n    for sid, x in nodes:\n        shifted.append(index[(sid, tuple((u + D // 2) % D for u in x))])\n    def near(i, j):\n        v = dist[i][j]\n        return v is not None and 8 * v <= D\n    raw = []\n    for outcome in a['outcomes']:\n        sid, x = outcome['space'], outcome['point']\n        require(type(sid) is int and 0 <= sid < len(spaces))\n        require(isinstance(x, list) and len(x) == spaces[sid])\n        require(all(type(u) is int and 0 <= u < D for u in x))\n        w = Fraction(outcome['weight'])\n        require(w >= 0)\n        raw.append((index[(sid, tuple(x))], w))\n    require(sum((w for _, w in raw), Fraction(0)) == 1)\n    if any(near(i, shifted[i]) for i in range(N)):\n        return {'branch':'clique', 'n':q, 'edges':[[i,j] for i in range(q) for j in range(i+1,q)]}\n    denominator = 1\n    for _, w in raw:\n        denominator = math.lcm(denominator, w.denominator)\n    locations = []\n    for location, w in raw:\n        copies = w.numerator * (denominator // w.denominator)\n        locations.extend([location] * copies)\n    edges = []\n    for i in range(len(locations)):\n        for j in range(i+1, len(locations)):\n            if near(locations[i], shifted[locations[j]]):\n                edges.append([i,j])\n    return {'branch':'metric', 'n':len(locations), 'edges':edges}\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n <a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The finite graph encoding can be implemented with exact arithmetic when the construction parameters and Label Cover alphabets are supplied as fixed data. Its output is an explicit graph, while a satisfying coloring is guaranteed rather than supplied. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The occurrences and phase draws define a deterministic hardness reduction through exact probability expansion. The paper supplies no interactive game mechanics, simulation dynamics, or simulation accuracy contract. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A graph benchmark generator can use the explicit encoding with fixed supplied parameters. Formulas whose satisfiability status is independently known distinguish completeness examples from soundness examples, subject to the reduction's promises. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | stated | The paper gives a deterministic finite reduction and exact shortest-path implementation of its geometric tests. This is an algorithm for constructing hardness instances, rather than an algorithm for finding colorings or maximum independent sets. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The construction provides a concrete representation of phase grids linked by projection pullbacks. The auxiliary weighted structure and the final simple graph must remain distinct, and output vertices retain multiplicity even when their locations coincide. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The graph encoding uses finite enumeration and exact distance comparisons, but no circuit construction, device mapping, hardware cost model, or accelerator resource bound is supplied. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The stated reduction establishes a graph promise hardness result. It does not specify a cryptographic primitive, secret distribution, adversary model, or security reduction for a protocol. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The junta theorem approximates Lipschitz functions by coordinate conditional expectations. The paper does not provide a learning procedure from examples, a sample-access model, or a predictive generalization guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | For rational alignment tolerance, the paper gives a terminating search that enumerates finite equality patterns and minimizes their maximum bad-set probability by rational linear programming. This constructs an alignment distribution; it does not solve graph coloring or independent-set optimization. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The fixed-palette hardness theorem can inform an abstract conflict-graph model, but the paper supplies no distributed scheduling construction, communication protocol, execution semantics, or message complexity contract. |
| other (Graph theory and computational complexity) | True | strong | implementable | stated | The explicit graph construction realizes a completeness and independence-density gap. A satisfying Label Cover labeling also provides a concrete all-vertex coloring by evaluating product answers and assigning the resulting phases to the specified half-open circle intervals. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-fixed-density-nonuniformity** (formal_scope): Polynomial time and output size are guaranteed for each fixed density threshold. The paper does not assert a uniform runtime or a procedure computing all construction constants from a varying threshold. Blocked use: ['Treating the density threshold as an additional input while retaining the stated polynomial guarantee.', 'Presenting existence of fixed construction data as an efficient parameter-generation algorithm.']
- **limit-fixed-palette-range** (hypothesis): The coloring hardness consequence requires fixed promised and target palettes satisfying the stated ordering condition. Blocked use: ['Extending the corollary to palettes that grow with the graph size.', 'Applying the corollary to a smaller promised palette outside its stated range.']
- **limit-completeness-coloring-not-output** (formal_scope): The reduction guarantees a proper coloring of every completeness vertex but does not supply that coloring with the graph. Blocked use: ['Using graph generation as a satisfying-labeling search algorithm.', 'Advertising the reduction as a constructive coloring solver without a supplied satisfying labeling.']
- **limit-explicit-occurrences-and-projections** (hypothesis): The source constraint instance uses nonempty finite alphabets, a nonempty explicit occurrence list, total projection maps, and uniform sampling of occurrences including repetitions. Blocked use: ['Replacing the explicit occurrence list by an unspecified sampling interface.', 'Discarding repeated occurrences or using partial answer maps without re-establishing the source contract.']
- **limit-fixed-encoding-parameters** (hypothesis): The deterministic polynomial encoding bound assumes fixed construction parameters and fixed Label Cover alphabets. Blocked use: ['Claiming the same polynomial bound when grid dimensions, alphabet sizes, and layer counts vary freely with the input.', 'Inferring practical graph sizes or resource requirements from the qualitative polynomial guarantee.']
- **limit-preprocessing-and-trivial-branches** (edge_case): The formula reduction handles clause preprocessing and separate trivial output branches before applying the nontrivial constraint construction. Blocked use: ['Invoking the nonempty occurrence-list construction directly on every raw formula.', 'Omitting the satisfiable clause-free branch or the unsatisfiable empty-clause branch.']
- **limit-exact-metric-and-clique-test** (hypothesis): The auxiliary geometry requires an even grid denominator, zero-length projection links, exact distance comparisons, and a clique test over auxiliary points. Distinct points can have zero distance, while disconnected components have infinite distance. Blocked use: ['Rounding threshold comparisons or replacing infinity by a value that passes a finite threshold.', 'Skipping the clique test or treating auxiliary projection links as output edges.']
- **limit-exact-probability-multiplicities** (formal_scope): The soundness passage from sampling probabilities to independent-set density relies on exact rational expansion into distinct unweighted vertices. Coincident locations do not justify merging vertices. Blocked use: ['Sampling a representative subset of outcomes instead of constructing the exact expanded graph.', 'Deduplicating vertices by location or assigning equal multiplicity to outcomes with unequal probabilities.']
- **limit-perfect-completeness-labeling** (hypothesis): The all-vertex coloring proof assumes a labeling satisfying every test occurrence. Approximate satisfaction does not provide the same agreement across every projection link. Blocked use: ['Applying the completeness coloring argument to an instance whose value is merely close to perfect.', 'Claiming all-vertex completeness from an almost-satisfying labeling.']
- **limit-continuous-junta-selection** (nonconstructive): The coordinate lists approximate continuous functions in the soundness proof. Their existence and ordered selection rule do not supply an executable approximation-error decision procedure, and the reduction never computes these lists. Blocked use: ['Treating the list-selection argument as an efficient feature-extraction routine.', 'Requiring the graph generator to compute the analytic juntas.']
- **limit-subchain-locality-and-source-soundness** (hypothesis): The separated-list collision bound uses low source value and a list rule depending only on the stated subchain data, fixed before the separating occurrence is sampled. Blocked use: ['Allowing a candidate list to depend on the opposite question or the projection of the separating occurrence.', 'Using the collision bound without the source soundness assumption.']
- **limit-universal-alignment-law** (hypothesis): The alignment law is selected before the list family and independently of the label universe. Its guarantee assumes bounded lists and disjointness for separated index sets; common-index alignment is stronger than pairwise intersection information. Blocked use: ["Choosing a different sampling law after inspecting each independent set's lists.", 'Replacing common-index alignment by pairwise overlap checks.', 'Applying the alignment guarantee without separated disjointness.']
- **limit-alignment-search-effectivity** (formal_scope): For rational positive tolerance, the finite alignment data admit a terminating enumeration and rational optimization search. The paper supplies no runtime bound for that search. Blocked use: ['Describing the alignment search as polynomial-time parameter generation.', 'Confusing finite search effectivity with uniform efficiency of the complete reduction.']
- **limit-coefficient-and-grid-error-budget** (approximation): The aligned density estimate assumes every stated parameter inequality. Zero-coefficient deletion, positive-level influence control, phase absorption, and grid coupling each contribute conditions that cannot be dropped independently. Blocked use: ['Applying the aligned density estimate to arbitrary supplied coefficients and grids.', 'Absorbing auxiliary phases into a fractional coefficient as though it were a full coefficient.', 'Ignoring compatibility error when deleting the minimum selected layer.']
- **limit-parameter-dependency-order** (hypothesis): The list bound is fixed before the layer count, source soundness, and answer alphabets. The reduction's parameter argument relies on this independence. Blocked use: ['Allowing the list capacity to depend on the alphabets that are chosen only after the source soundness.', 'Using circular parameter choices as justification for the density gap.']
- **limit-independence-density-versus-colorability** (formal_scope): A chromatic-number lower bound alone does not preserve the independent-set density promise. Adding isolated vertices can destroy the density gap while preserving chromatic number. Blocked use: ['Replacing the independent-set soundness guarantee by noncolorability and retaining the density conclusion.', 'Adding arbitrary isolated vertices to hardness instances while claiming the original independence ratio.']
- **limit-finite-kernel-certificate** (formal_scope): Finite tests of supplied phase spaces, projection maps, and outcome probabilities can assess an encoding specialization. They do not establish the complete formula reduction or its continuous analytic soundness proof. Blocked use: ['Interpreting agreement on finite encoding examples as certification of the complete hardness theorem.', 'Claiming the supplied outcome law itself was generated by the full occurrence-chain construction.']

An occurrence $c$ has questions $u_c\\in U$, $v_c\\in V$, and a total map $\\pi_c:\\Sigma_L\\to\\Sigma_R$. The value is $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{val}}(\\mathcal C)=\n \\max_{\\ell:U\\to\\Sigma_L,\\ \\rho:V\\to\\Sigma_R}\n \\Pr_{c\\in\\mathcal C}\\bigl[\\pi_c(\\ell(u_c))=\\rho(v_c)\\bigr],$$ where occurrences, including repeated occurrences, are sampled uniformly.\n\n\n<div id=\"thm:label-cover\" class=\"theorem\">\n\n**Theorem 3** (Perfect-completeness Label Cover). *For every fixed rational $\\sigma\\in(0,1)$ there are nonempty constant alphabets and a deterministic polynomial-time reduction from $3$SAT to explicit Label Cover instances with nonempty occurrence lists such that satisfiable formulas have value $1$ and unsatisfiable formulas have value at most $\\sigma$.*\n\n</div>\n\nThis is the standard consequence of the PCP Theorem and parallel repetition (Arora et al. 1998; Raz 1998) [[AroraEtAl1998](#ref-AroraEtAl1998), [Raz1998](#ref-Raz1998)]. Choose a fixed $k$ for which $\\beta_0^k\\le\\sigma$. \"evaluate\" on the right.\n- Horizontal equality sign \"$=$\" between $x(\\pi(\\kappa_i))$ and $x(\\kappa_{i+1})$, with label below: \"$\\pi(\\kappa_i) = \\kappa_{i+1}$\".\nRight diagram (circle geometry):\n- A circle partitioned into three colored arcs representing the half-open color intervals:\n  - Green arc covering $[0, 1/3)$, with label $0$ at 3 o'clock and label $1/3$ at top (around 12 o'clock).\n  - Blue arc covering $[1/3, 2/3)$, ending at label $2/3$ at lower left (around 7–8 o'clock).\n  - Red arc covering $[2/3, 1)$ completing the circle to $0$.\n- Point labeled $\\varphi(x)$ on the green arc (around 2 o'clock).\n- Dashed diameter line extending from $\\varphi(x)$ across the circle to the antipodal point labeled $\\varphi(x) + \\frac{1}{2}$ on the blue arc.\n- A thick black outer arc concentric with the circle centered around $\\varphi(x) + \\frac{1}{2}$, indicating the range of possible phases $\\varphi(y)$ of its neighbors within $1/8$ of the antipodal phase.\n- Text below the circle: \"$d_{\\mathbb{T}}(\\varphi(x), \\varphi(y)) \\ge \\frac{3}{8}$\" and \"for every output edge $xy$\".\n```\n\n\n\n### From an independent set to bounded subchain lists\n\nFix $\\sigma\\in(0,1)$ and assume that the Label Cover value is at most $\\sigma$. The clique branch already has the required soundness, so suppose it does not occur. Fix a nonempty independent vertex set $\\mathcal A$ in the output graph. We first turn it into functions on the tuple tori, then approximate their restrictions to small subchains using bounded coordinate lists. Source soundness will show that lists belonging to separated subchains are usually disjoint. Throughout the analysis, set $L=64$.\n\n\n#### Odd functions and compatibility\n\n\nLet $A\\subseteq\\mathscr X$ be the set of locations of vertices in $\\mathcal A$, with multiplicities removed. Then $$\\label{eq:independent-separation}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(A,TA)>1/8.$$ For locations of distinct selected vertices this follows from the missing edge in <a href=\"#eq:edge\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:edge\">[eq:edge]</a>. For a point compared with itself, it follows from exclusion of <a href=\"#eq:clique-test\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:clique-test\">[eq:clique-test]</a>. Since the sets are finite, these pointwise strict inequalities give <a href=\"#eq:independent-separation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:independent-separation\">[eq:independent-separation]</a>, including when distances are infinite.\n\nOn $\\mathscr X$ define $$\\label{eq:bumps}\n f(x)=\\bigl(1-32\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,A)\\bigr)_+\n       -\\bigl(1-32\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(Tx,A)\\bigr)_+,\n \\qquad (u)_+=\\max\\{u,0\\},$$ with each bump defined to be zero at infinite distance. This function takes values in $[-1,1]$, satisfies $f(Tx)=-f(x)$, and equals $1$ on $A$. It is constant across every zero link. Within each grid copy it is $64$-Lipschitz for the sup circle metric: distance to $A$ is $1$-Lipschitz on a component meeting $A$, and its bump is identically zero on a component not meeting $A$. Also the shortest-path pseudometric never exceeds a within-copy link distance.\n\nAt each tuple $\\mathbf q$, extend its grid function to the whole torus $\\mathbb T^{M_i}$. Using the real-valued Lipschitz extension formula of McShane (McShane 1934) [[McShane1934](#ref-McShane1934)], set $$g_{\\mathbf q}(x)=\n \\min_{y\\in\\Gamma^{M_i}}\n       \\bigl(f(y)+L\\,d_\\infty(x,y)\\bigr),$$ where $y$ ranges over that tuple’s copy and $d_\\infty$ is the sup circle metric. This is $L$-Lipschitz and equals $f$ on the grid. Clip its values to $[-1,1]$, obtaining $\\widetilde g_{\\mathbf q}$, and put $$F_{\\mathbf q}(x)=\n \\frac{\\widetilde g_{\\mathbf q}(x)\n       -\\widetilde g_{\\mathbf q}(Tx)}2.$$ The resulting function is still an extension of the odd grid data, is $L$-Lipschitz, is valued in $[-1,1]$, and is odd under $T$. Fix this entire family of functions before sampling any test chain. Along a chain write $F_i=F_{\\mathbf q_i}$.\n\n\nFor every chain and every $i\\le j$, these extensions satisfy $$\\label{eq:compatibility}\n |F_j(x)-F_i(x\\circ\\pi_{ij})|\n \\le 2L/D,\\qquad x\\in\\mathbb T^{M_j}.$$ Indeed, round each coordinate of $x$ once to a grid point $y$ within $1/D$. Pullback does not enlarge this error. The grid points $y$ and $y\\circ\\pi_{ij}$ have equal $f$ values by the chain of zero links, so the two extension errors total at most $2L/D$. Intermediate layers contribute no additional approximation error.\n\n\n#### Bounded lists for a subchain\n\nThe functions $F_i$ now supply the analytic data we need along any chain. For each small set of layers, we will choose a bounded list of answer coordinates that approximates every allowed coefficient choice. The bound must be independent of the Label Cover alphabets, and the choice must depend only on the data of that subchain.\n\n\nFix an approximation tolerance $\\gamma>0$. For $\\varnothing\\ne I\\subseteq[r]$ with $|I|\\le s$, put $b_I=\\min I$. Given $t\\in\\mathcal T_m^I$, define $$\\label{eq:subchain-functions}\n h_{I,t}(\\theta)\n =F_{b_I}\\left(\n  \\left(\\sum_{j\\in I}t_j\\theta_{j,\\pi_{b_Ij}(\\kappa)}\n            \\pmod1\\right)_{\\kappa\\in M_{b_I}}\\right),\n \\qquad\n \\theta\\in\\prod_{j\\in I}[0,1]^{M_j}.$$ The entries of $\\theta$ are independent uniform real variables. This evaluates $F_{b_I}$ at the continuous version of the sampled location in <a href=\"#eq:location\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:location\">[eq:location]</a>, using only the layers of $I$. The phase map has Lipschitz constant at most $|I|\\le s$: coordinate duplication by a projection cannot enlarge a sup distance. Thus $h_{I,t}$ is $sL$-Lipschitz, independently of the label-set sizes and projection fibers.\n\n\n<div id=\"lem:subchain-lists\" class=\"lemma\">\n\n\n**Lemma 8** (Bounded subchain lists). *For every fixed occurrence chain and every nonempty $I\\subseteq[r]$ with $|I|\\le s$, there are sets $S_I^j\\subseteq M_j$, $j\\in I$, with $$\\label{eq:list-size}\n \\sum_{j\\in I}|S_I^j|\\le d,\n \\qquad d:=\\max\\{1,J(sL,\\gamma)(m+1)^s\\},$$ such that, for every $t\\in\\mathcal T_m^I$, a junta $g_{I,t}$ using only scalar coordinates $(j,\\ell)$ with $\\ell\\in S_I^j$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:subchain-junta}\n \\|h_{I,t}-g_{I,t}\\|_2<\\gamma.$$ The lists can be chosen as functions only of the subchain’s label sets, internal projection maps, and functions $F_j$, $j\\in I$. Identical subchain data receive identical choices.*\n\n</div>\n\n<details id=\"proof-stmt-lem-subchain-lists\"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-subchain-lists</summary>\n\n*Proof.* For each of the at most $(m+1)^s$ coefficient choices, apply Theorem <a href=\"#thm:junta\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:junta\">4</a> to $h_{I,t}$ with error $\\gamma$. This uses at most $J(sL,\\gamma)$ scalar coordinates. For each block $j$, take the union of its selected coordinates over all coefficient choices to obtain $S_I^j$ and <a href=\"#eq:list-size\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:list-size\">[eq:list-size]</a>.\n\nTo make the choice depend only on the stated subchain data, order the scalar coordinates and choose the first subset of size at most $J(sL,\\gamma)$ whose coordinate conditional expectation has error less than $\\gamma$. The theorem guarantees such a subset. These choices enter only the soundness proof and are never computed by the reduction. In particular, a list includes approximating coordinates for every coefficient vector before any coefficients are sampled. ◻\n\n</details>\n\n\n#### Why the lists can be decoded locally\n\n\nUse the fixed rule of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:subchain-lists\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:subchain-lists\">8</a> along a uniform occurrence chain. Using $M_r=\\Sigma_R^{r-1}$ as a common label universe, set $$\\label{eq:terminal-lists}\n A_I=\\bigcup_{j\\in I}\\pi_{jr}(S_I^j),\n \\qquad \\varnothing\\ne I\\subseteq[r],\\quad |I|\\le s.$$ By <a href=\"#eq:list-size\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:list-size\">[eq:list-size]</a>, each $A_I$ has size at most $d$. We first control intersections between lists on separated sets of layers.\n\n\n<div id=\"lem:separated-lists\" class=\"lemma\">\n\n**Lemma 9** (Separated lists). *For every fixed $I,J$ with $1\\le|I|,|J|\\le s$ and $\\max I<\\min J$, $$\\Pr_{\\text{chain}}(A_I\\cap A_J\\ne\\varnothing)\\le d^2\\sigma.$$*\n\n</div>\n\n<details id=\"proof-stmt-lem-separated-lists\"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-separated-lists</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Put $h=\\max I<\\min J$ and condition on every occurrence except $c_h$. The remaining occurrence is still uniform in the original instance. At each layer in $I$, position $h$ of the tuple is the left question $u_h$. Every internal projection between layers of $I$ uses only tests with index less than $h$. Thus all subchain data for $I$, including its already fixed tuple functions, depend only on $u_h$ and the conditioned background, not on the opposite question or on the projection of $c_h$. Likewise, all data for $J$ depend only on $v_h$ and the background. Figure <a href=\"#fig:locality\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"fig:locality\">2</a> illustrates this separation.\n\n<figure id=\"fig:locality\">\n\n<figcaption>Four layers with the separating test $c_{2}$ left unconditioned. Once $c_{1}, c_{3}$ are fixed, the left and right subchain data depend only on the respective own question of $c_{2}$. Answer maps act in the position indicated by each test.</figcaption>\n</figure>\n\n\n**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:locality`)\n\nTikZ/PGF source, copied verbatim from the source:\n\n```latex\n\\begin{tikzpicture}[>=Stealth, every node/.style={font=\\small}]\n\\node (a) at (0,0) {$(u_1,u_2,u_3)$};\n\\node (b) at (3.8,0) {$(v_1,u_2,u_3)$};\n\\node (c) at (7.6,0) {$(v_1,v_2,u_3)$};\n\\node (d) at (11.4,0) {$(v_1,v_2,v_3)$};\n\\draw[->] (a) -- node[above] {$c_1$} (b);\n\\draw[->] (b) -- node[above] {$c_2$} (c);\n\\draw[->] (c) -- node[above] {$c_3$} (d);\n\\draw[dashed,gray] (5.7,-0.9) -- (5.7,1.1);\n\\node at (1.9,-0.65) {Position $2$: own question $u_2$};\n\\node at (9.5,-0.65) {Position $2$: own question $v_2$};\n\\node[font=\\footnotesize] at (1.9,0.9) {left subchain};\n\\node[font=\\footnotesize] at (9.5,0.9) {right subchain};\n\\end{tikzpicture}\n```\n\nVisual witness description of PDF page 11 (generated from the PDF page, not source text):\n\n```text\nA horizontal sequence of four coordinate tuples connected by directed arrows:\n- Four nodes: $(u_1, u_2, u_3)$, $(v_1, u_2, u_3)$, $(v_1, v_2, u_3)$, and $(v_1, v_2, v_3)$.\n- Horizontal directed arrows between consecutive nodes:\n  - Arrow from $(u_1, u_2, u_3)$ to $(v_1, u_2, u_3)$ labeled $c_1$ above.\n  - Arrow from $(v_1, u_2, u_3)$ to $(v_1, v_2, u_3)$ labeled $c_2$ above.\n  - Arrow from $(v_1, v_2, u_3)$ to $(v_1, v_2, v_3)$ labeled $c_3$ above.\n- A vertical dashed line passing through the middle of the $c_2$ arrow, dividing the diagram into two subchains:\n  - Left pair labeled \"left subchain\" above.\n  - Right pair labeled \"right subchain\" above.\n- Text below:\n  - Under the left subchain: \"Position 2: own question $u_2$\".\n  - Under the right subchain: \"Position 2: own question $v_2$\".\n```\n\n\nForm a list of candidate left answers by taking position $h$ of every label in every $S_I^j$ for $j\\in I$. This gives at most $d$ elements of $\\Sigma_L$, as a function of $u_h$ and fixed background. The analogous list for $J$ gives at most $d$ elements of $\\Sigma_R$ as a function of $v_h$. Choose deterministic orderings and pad each list to length $d$ with an arbitrary answer, also when the list is empty.\n\nIf $A_I$ and $A_J$ intersect, some left and right selected labels have the same image at layer $r$. Equality in position $h$ says that $\\pi_{c_h}$ sends the associated left candidate to the right candidate: on the left, exactly the test $c_h$ acts in that position, and on the right the position is already a right-answer coordinate. For each pair of list positions, selecting those answers for each own question is a deterministic strategy for the original Label Cover instance. Its success probability over the uniform $c_h$ is at most $\\sigma$. A union bound over the $d^2$ position pairs proves the conditional bound, and averaging over the background proves the lemma. ◻\n\n</details>\n\nThe fixed functions may depend on the entire instance and on $\\mathcal A$. This causes no loss of locality: they are fixed before the sampling experiment, so evaluating the family at a tuple reveals only that tuple. In particular, if two occurrences have the same own question but different opposite questions or projections, the list rule at that side uses identical data.\n\nWe have obtained bounded lists in a common answer set, and source soundness controls intersections of lists on separated layer sets. The next section turns this separation into a stronger property: each listed answer can be assigned to one layer common to all of its occurrences inside the sampled set $B$.\n\n\n### Aligning the lists on a sampled set of layers\n\nThe terminal lists $A_I\\subseteq M_r$ all have size at most $d$. For the analytic argument, we need more than disjointness on separated sets: each label should have a layer common to every set on whose list it appears. These properties differ even for one label. A label that occurs on the three sets $\\{1,2\\}$, $\\{1,3\\}$, and $\\{2,3\\}$ has no common index, although none of these sets is separated from another.\n\nThe following abstract lemma supplies the common-index property on a sampled $s$-element set. Its essential quantifier is that the sampling law is chosen before the lists, with no dependence on the label universe. This is what allows the same graph construction to handle every independent set.\n\n<div class=\"samepage\">\n\n\n<div id=\"lem:alignment\" class=\"lemma\">\n\n\n\n**Lemma 10** (Distributional alignment). *For every pair of integers $s,d\\geq 1$ and every real $\\eta>0$, there exist an integer $r\\geq s$ and a rational probability distribution $\\mu$ on $\\binom{[r]}s$ with the following property.Let $M_*$ be any set, and let $A_I\\subseteq M_*$, $|A_I|\\leq d$, be specified for every nonempty $I\\subseteq[r]$ with $|I|\\leq s$. Assume that $$\\label{align:separation}\n A_I\\cap A_J=\\varnothing\n \\qquad\\text{whenever }\\max I<\\min J.$$ Then, with probability at least $1-\\eta$ over $B\\sim\\mu$, there exists a map $a:M_*\\to B$ such that $$\\label{align:conclusion}\n a(A_I)\\subseteq I\n \\qquad\\text{for every nonempty }I\\subseteq B.$$ The map $a$ may depend on the list family and on $B$.*\n\n</div>\n\n</div>\n\nThe complete proof is in Section <a href=\"#sec:alignment-proof\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:alignment-proof\">8</a>. It replaces labels by their finite equality patterns, applies minimax and Ramsey homogenization, and obtains an order-invariant random pattern. In that limit, separated disjointness forces every label to have an index common to all of its occurrences. We now apply the lemma to the lists already extracted from an independent set.\n\nFix $\\varepsilon>0$, and suppose that the layer count $r$ and law $\\mu$ in the graph construction are supplied by Lemma <a href=\"#lem:alignment\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:alignment\">10</a> for $s,d,\\eta=\\varepsilon$. Section <a href=\"#sec:parameters\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:parameters\">7</a> will choose these data after the alphabet-independent bound $d$ is fixed and before choosing the Label Cover instance.\n\n\n<div class=\"definition\">\n\n**Definition 11**. A pair consisting of a chain and a set $B\\in\\binom{[r]}s$ is *aligned* if there is a map $a:M_r\\to B$ such that $a(A_I)\\subseteq I$ for every nonempty $I\\subseteq B$.\n\n</div>\n\n\n<div id=\"lem:good-pairs\" class=\"lemma\">\n\n**Lemma 12** (Aligned pairs occur with high probability). *Under these choices, for independent uniform chain sampling and $B\\sim\\mu$, the probability that the pair is not aligned is at most $4^r d^2\\sigma+\\varepsilon$.*\n\n</div>\n\n<details id=\"proof-stmt-lem-good-pairs\"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-good-pairs</summary>\n\n*Proof.* There are at most $4^r$ ordered pairs of subsets of $[r]$. By Lemma <a href=\"#lem:separated-lists\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:separated-lists\">9</a> and a union bound, the probability that some separated pair of terminal lists intersects is at most $4^r d^2\\sigma$. For each chain with no such intersection, Lemma <a href=\"#lem:alignment\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:alignment\">10</a> gives an alignment except for a set of $B$ of $\\mu$-probability at most $\\varepsilon$. Adding the two failure probabilities proves the bound. ◻\n\n</details>\n\nThe lists were selected before $B$, and include the juntas for every coefficient choice. An alignment for a fixed aligned pair can therefore be chosen before the actual coefficient and rotation draws. The next section uses this fixed alignment to control dependence on one auxiliary phase for each layer in $B$.\n\n\n### The coefficient test and soundness\n\nThe remaining task is to bound the density of an independent set after fixing an aligned pair of a chain and a selected set of layers. We add one auxiliary phase for each selected layer. Alignment makes the influence of a zero-coefficient layer small, whereas the geometric coefficient distribution makes large influences at positive coefficients unlikely.\n\n\nKeep the tolerance $\\varepsilon>0$ of Section <a href=\"#sec:alignment\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:alignment\">5</a>, and take $\\beta>0$ and $K\\ge1$ from Lemma <a href=\"#lem:influences\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:influences\">5</a> with $L=64$ and this $\\varepsilon$. For the graph parameters of Section <a href=\"#sec:construction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:construction\">3</a> and the list-approximation tolerance $\\gamma$ of Section <a href=\"#sec:lists\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:lists\">4</a>, assume $$\\label{eq:parameter-bounds}\n \\begin{gathered}\n L/m<\\beta/2,\\qquad \\lambda K<\\varepsilon,\n \\qquad (1-p_m)^s<\\varepsilon,\\\\\n \\frac{s(2\\gamma)^2}{\\beta^2}<\\varepsilon,\\qquad\n \\frac{2L}{D}<\\gamma,\\qquad \\frac{2Ls}{P}<\\varepsilon.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Section <a href=\"#sec:parameters\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:parameters\">7</a> will choose constants satisfying these inequalities. A coefficient equal to $1$ is called a *full coefficient*. Although its individual probability $p_m$ may be small, the third inequality makes at least one of the $s$ coefficients full with probability greater than $1-\\varepsilon$.\n\n\n<div id=\"prop:aligned-bound\" class=\"proposition\">\n\n**Proposition 13**. *Assume <a href=\"#eq:parameter-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:parameter-bounds\">[eq:parameter-bounds]</a>. Suppose that the construction does not take the clique branch, and let $\\mathcal A$ be a nonempty independent set of its output graph. Form the functions and lists associated with $\\mathcal A$ as above. For every chain and $s$-element set $B$ admitting a map $$a:M_r\\longrightarrow B,\n \\qquad a(A_I)\\subseteq I\n \\quad\\text{for every nonempty }I\\subseteq B,$$ the conditional probability that the sampled output vertex belongs to $\\mathcal A$ is at most $6\\varepsilon$.*\n\n</div>\n\n<details id=\"proof-stmt-prop-aligned-bound\"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-aligned-bound</summary>\n\n\n\n*Proof.* Fix such a chain, $B$, and a map $a$, before drawing any coefficients or rotations. Write $b=\\min B$. Since $f=1$ at every location of $\\mathcal A$, it suffices to bound the expected square of $F_b$ at the sampled grid location by $6\\varepsilon$. We establish this through a continuous rotation experiment with auxiliary phases. Initially replace the discrete rotations by independent uniform real variables $$\\theta_{j,\\ell}\\in[0,1),\n \\qquad j\\in B,\\quad \\ell\\in M_j.$$ All these variables are independent of the coefficient vector $t\\in\\mathcal T_m^B$. Introduce additional independent Haar-uniform variables $z=(z_i)_{i\\in B}\\in\\mathbb T^B$, and define $$\\label{coeff:H}\n H(t,\\theta;z)\n =F_b\\left(\\left(\n z_{a(\\pi_{br}(\\kappa))}\n +\\sum_{j\\in B}t_j\\theta_{j,\\pi_{bj}(\\kappa)}\n \\pmod 1\\right)_{\\kappa\\in M_b}\\right).$$ For fixed $t,\\theta$, the map from $z$ to the phase vector in <a href=\"#coeff:H\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"coeff:H\">[coeff:H]</a> is nonexpanding for the sup circle metrics: each output coordinate uses just one coordinate of $z$. Consequently $H$ is $L$-Lipschitz as a function of $z$, independently of $s$ and of all label cardinalities. It is bounded by one in absolute value. A simultaneous half-shift of the $z$ coordinates half-shifts every input coordinate of $F_b$, so oddness gives $$\\label{coeff:mean-zero}\n \\mathbb E_z H(t,\\theta;z)=0.$$\n\n\n\nLet $E_i$ average only $z_i$ and let $Q_i=\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}-E_i$, an orthogonal projection of norm one. We use the notation $$D_i(t,\\theta)=\\|Q_iH(t,\\theta;\\cdot)\\|_{L^2(\\mathbb T^B)},\n \\qquad X(t,\\theta)=\\mathbb E_z H(t,\\theta;z)^2.$$ By Lemma <a href=\"#lem:influences\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:influences\">5</a> and <a href=\"#coeff:mean-zero\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"coeff:mean-zero\">[coeff:mean-zero]</a>, $$\\begin{aligned}\n X(t,\\theta)>\\varepsilon\n &\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\max_{i\\in B}D_i(t,\\theta)\\ge\\beta,\n \\label{coeff:variance-detection}\\\\\n \\#\\{i\\in B:D_i(t,\\theta)\\ge\\beta/2\\}\n &\\le K\n \\quad\\text{for every }t,\\theta.\n \\label{coeff:count}\n\\end{aligned}$$\n\n\n###### Zero coefficients.\n\n\nLet $$\\mathcal F=\\{t:\\text{some }t_j=1\\}$$ be the event that a full coefficient is present. Fix $t\\in\\mathcal F$ and an index $i\\in B$ with $t_i=0$. Choose $j\\in B$ with $t_j=1$; necessarily $j\\ne i$. Put $I=B\\setminus\\{i\\}$ and $c=\\min I$. The set $I$ is nonempty. In the subchain function $h_{I,t|_I}$ from <a href=\"#eq:subchain-functions\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:subchain-functions\">[eq:subchain-functions]</a>, leave all rotation blocks unchanged except block $j$, where we substitute $$\\label{coeff:shift}\n \\theta'_{j,\\ell}\n =\\theta_{j,\\ell}+z_{a(\\pi_{jr}(\\ell))}\\pmod 1,\n \\qquad \\ell\\in M_j.$$ Here and below a phase used as a real rotation is represented in $[0,1)$. For each fixed $z$, this substitution preserves the product uniform distribution of the rotation inputs. Even when several coordinates receive the same shift, each coordinate undergoes a fixed translation, so their conditional joint law remains the same product law.\n\n\nTo compare the two function evaluations, let $y\\in\\mathbb T^{M_c}$ be the phase vector in $h_{I,t|_I}(\\theta')$. For every $\\kappa\\in M_b$, composition of the projections gives $$\\label{coeff:pullback-identity}\n y_{\\pi_{bc}(\\kappa)}\n =z_{a(\\pi_{br}(\\kappa))}\n   +\\sum_{k\\in B}t_k\\theta_{k,\\pi_{bk}(\\kappa)}\n   \\pmod 1.$$ Indeed, the omitted $i$ term is zero, and the changed $j$ term has coefficient exactly one. This last fact is essential: reducing the shifted rotation modulo one before multiplying would not in general be valid at a fractional coefficient. Thus $H(t,\\theta;z)=F_b(y\\circ\\pi_{bc})$. If $c=b$ the two evaluations are identical; if deleting $i$ changes the minimum layer, the compatibility bound <a href=\"#eq:compatibility\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:compatibility\">[eq:compatibility]</a> still gives $$\\label{coeff:deletion-error}\n |H(t,\\theta;z)-h_{I,t|_I}(\\theta')|\n \\le 2L/D<\\gamma.$$\n\n\nLet $g_{I,t|_I}$ be the fixed junta approximating $h_{I,t|_I}$ in $L^2$ norm to error less than $\\gamma$. Its selected coordinates in block $k$ belong to $S_I^k$. Product-law preservation for every fixed $z$ implies $$\\big\\|h_{I,t|_I}(\\theta')-g_{I,t|_I}(\\theta')\\big\\|_{L^2(\\theta,z)}\n <\\gamma.$$ Moreover, the composed function $g_{I,t|_I}(\\theta')$ is pointwise independent of $z_i$. Only the modified block $j$ introduces any $z$ dependence. Every selected coordinate $\\ell\\in S_I^j$ satisfies $\\pi_{jr}(\\ell)\\in A_I$ by <a href=\"#eq:terminal-lists\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:terminal-lists\">[eq:terminal-lists]</a>, and alignment therefore gives $a(\\pi_{jr}(\\ell))\\in I$. Combining this observation with <a href=\"#coeff:deletion-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"coeff:deletion-error\">[coeff:deletion-error]</a> and applying the contraction $Q_i$ on the full product space yields $$\\label{coeff:zero-bound}\n \\mathbb E_\\theta D_i(t,\\theta)^2\n =\\|Q_iH\\|_{L^2(\\theta,z)}^2\n \\le\n \\big\\|H-g_{I,t|_I}(\\theta')\\big\\|_{L^2(\\theta,z)}^2\n <(2\\gamma)^2.$$ This estimate holds for every fixed $t\\in\\mathcal F$ with $t_i=0$. Markov’s inequality, followed by a union bound over the $s$ indices, therefore gives $$\\label{coeff:zero-probability}\n \\Pr_{t,\\theta}\\bigl(\n t\\in\\mathcal F,\\ \n \\exists i\\in B:\\ t_i=0,\\ D_i(t,\\theta)\\ge\\beta\n \\bigr)\n \\le \\frac{s(2\\gamma)^2}{\\beta^2}<\\varepsilon.$$\n\n\n###### Positive coefficients.\n\n\n\nWe next bound large influences at positive coefficient levels. This step does not assume that a full coefficient is present. Fix $i$, all rotations $\\theta$, and all coefficients other than $t_i$. For $0\\le k\\le m$, let $H_k$ denote <a href=\"#coeff:H\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"coeff:H\">[coeff:H]</a> with $t_i=k/m$, and put $d_k=\\|Q_iH_k\\|_2$. Changing one coefficient by $1/m$ changes each phase coordinate by circle distance at most $1/m$, since the rotations lie in $[0,1)$. The norm-one property of $Q_i$ gives $$\\label{coeff:one-step}\n |d_k-d_{k-1}|\n \\le\\|Q_i(H_k-H_{k-1})\\|_2\n \\le L/m<\\beta/2\n \\qquad(1\\le k\\le m).$$ Write $p_k=\\lambda^k/\\sum_{h=0}^m\\lambda^h$ for the probability of coefficient level $k/m$. Since $p_k=\\lambda p_{k-1}$, <a href=\"#coeff:one-step\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"coeff:one-step\">[coeff:one-step]</a> implies, for the fixed background data, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sum_{k=1}^m p_k\\mathbf 1_{\\{d_k\\ge\\beta\\}}\n &\\le\\lambda\\sum_{h=0}^{m-1}\n              p_h\\mathbf 1_{\\{d_h\\ge\\beta/2\\}}\\\\\n &\\le\\lambda\\sum_{h=0}^m\n              p_h\\mathbf 1_{\\{d_h\\ge\\beta/2\\}}.\n\\end{aligned}$$ Integrating over the fixed data and summing over $i\\in B$, we obtain from <a href=\"#coeff:count\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"coeff:count\">[coeff:count]</a> $$\\label{coeff:positive-probability}\n \\begin{split}\n \\Pr_{t,\\theta}\\bigl(\n \\exists i\\in B:\\ t_i>0,\\ D_i(t,\\theta)\\ge\\beta\n \\bigr)\n &\\le\\lambda\\sum_{i\\in B}\n          \\Pr_{t,\\theta}(D_i(t,\\theta)\\ge\\beta/2)\\\\\n &\\le\\lambda K<\\varepsilon.\n \\end{split}$$ Decreasing a level can destroy the only full coefficient. This causes no restriction here: <a href=\"#coeff:count\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"coeff:count\">[coeff:count]</a> holds for every coefficient vector, including all images of the one-level decrease.\n\n\n###### From influences to mean square.\n\n\nThe parameter choices in <a href=\"#eq:parameter-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:parameter-bounds\">[eq:parameter-bounds]</a> give $\\Pr(t\\notin\\mathcal F)<\\varepsilon$. By <a href=\"#coeff:variance-detection\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"coeff:variance-detection\">[coeff:variance-detection]</a>, the event $X(t,\\theta)>\\varepsilon$ is contained in the union of this event and the two events bounded in <a href=\"#coeff:zero-probability\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"coeff:zero-probability\">[coeff:zero-probability]</a> and <a href=\"#coeff:positive-probability\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"coeff:positive-probability\">[coeff:positive-probability]</a>. Consequently $$\\Pr_{t,\\theta}(X(t,\\theta)>\\varepsilon)<3\\varepsilon.$$ Since $0\\le X\\le1$, it follows that $$\\label{coeff:mean-square}\n \\mathbb E_{t,\\theta,z}H(t,\\theta;z)^2\n =\\mathbb E_{t,\\theta}X(t,\\theta)\n \\le\\varepsilon+\\Pr(X>\\varepsilon)<4\\varepsilon.$$ We now remove the auxiliary phases and return to the actual grid sampling. This is the only remaining passage from the analytic test to the conditional vertex density.\n\n\n###### Removing the phases and discretizing.\n\n\nDefine the continuous location $$x(t,\\theta)\n =\\left(\\sum_{j\\in B}t_j\\theta_{j,\\pi_{bj}(\\kappa)}\n          \\pmod 1\\right)_{\\kappa\\in M_b}.$$ For every fixed $t\\in\\mathcal F$, choose an index $j$ with $t_j=1$. Applying the translation <a href=\"#coeff:shift\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"coeff:shift\">[coeff:shift]</a> to this block absorbs every $z$ term in <a href=\"#coeff:H\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"coeff:H\">[coeff:H]</a>. For each fixed $z$ the translated rotation array again has exactly its original product law. Hence $$\\label{coeff:absorption}\n \\mathbb E_{\\theta,z}H(t,\\theta;z)^2\n =\\mathbb E_\\theta F_b(x(t,\\theta))^2\n \\qquad(t\\in\\mathcal F).$$ The choice of the full block may depend on $t$, because this equality is asserted separately for every fixed coefficient vector. In particular, <a href=\"#coeff:mean-square\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"coeff:mean-square\">[coeff:mean-square]</a> implies $$\\label{coeff:continuous-bound}\n \\mathbb E_{t,\\theta}\\!\\left[\n \\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal F}(t)F_b(x(t,\\theta))^2\\right]<4\\varepsilon.$$\n\n\n\nCouple the discrete rotations to the continuous ones by $$\\widehat\\theta_{j,\\ell}\n =\\frac{\\lfloor P\\theta_{j,\\ell}\\rfloor}{P}.$$ They have the required independent uniform grid law. Each input coordinate changes by less than $1/P$, so the sup circle distance between $x(t,\\theta)$ and $x(t,\\widehat\\theta)$ is at most $s/P$. The Lipschitz bound and the range $[-1,1]$ therefore imply $$\\label{coeff:grid-error}\n \\left|F_b(x(t,\\theta))^2\n       -F_b(x(t,\\widehat\\theta))^2\\right|\n \\le 2Ls/P<\\varepsilon.$$ Combining <a href=\"#coeff:continuous-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"coeff:continuous-bound\">[coeff:continuous-bound]</a>, <a href=\"#coeff:grid-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"coeff:grid-error\">[coeff:grid-error]</a>, and $\\Pr(t\\notin\\mathcal F)<\\varepsilon$ gives $$\\label{coeff:discrete-bound}\n \\mathbb E_{t,\\widehat\\theta}\n       F_b(x(t,\\widehat\\theta))^2\n <4\\varepsilon+\\varepsilon+\\varepsilon=6\\varepsilon.$$ The discrete location belongs to the grid of denominator $D=mP$, so $F_b$ equals the original grid function $f$ there. That function equals one at every location of a vertex of $\\mathcal A$. Thus the indicator that the sampled vertex belongs to $\\mathcal A$ is pointwise bounded by $F_b(x(t,\\widehat\\theta))^2$. This remains true when several distinct vertices have the same location. Inequality <a href=\"#coeff:discrete-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"coeff:discrete-bound\">[coeff:discrete-bound]</a> proves the proposition. ◻\n\n</details>\n\n\n### Choosing the constants and completing the reduction\n\nThe construction and its analysis have identified all the required properties of the fixed parameters. We now choose them in an order that makes the graph reduction possible. The key point is that the list bound $d$ is fixed before the layer count and the source soundness, so it cannot depend on the Label Cover alphabets.\n\nFix a rational $0<\\delta<1/3$ and a rational $0<\\varepsilon<\\delta/8$. Set $L=64$ and take $\\beta,K$ from Lemma <a href=\"#lem:influences\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:influences\">5</a> for $L,\\varepsilon$. Choose a positive integer $m$ with $L/m<\\beta/2$, then a rational $0<\\lambda<1$ with $\\lambda K<\\varepsilon$. The coefficient law <a href=\"#eq:coefficient-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:coefficient-law\">[eq:coefficient-law]</a> is now fixed. Since its probability $p_m$ of a full coefficient is positive, choose an integer $s\\ge2$ with $(1-p_m)^s<\\varepsilon$.\n\n\nNext choose $\\gamma>0$ sufficiently small that $s(2\\gamma)^2/\\beta^2<\\varepsilon$, and then choose an even positive integer $P$ sufficiently large that, with $D=mP$, $$\\frac{2L}{D}<\\gamma,\n \\qquad \\frac{2Ls}{P}<\\varepsilon.$$ This establishes every inequality in <a href=\"#eq:parameter-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:parameter-bounds\">[eq:parameter-bounds]</a>. Lemma <a href=\"#lem:subchain-lists\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:subchain-lists\">8</a> gives the alphabet-independent bound $$d=\\max\\{1,J(sL,\\gamma)(m+1)^s\\}.$$ Apply Lemma <a href=\"#lem:alignment\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:alignment\">10</a> to $s,d,\\eta=\\varepsilon$, obtaining $r\\ge s$ and a rational law $\\mu$ on $\\binom{[r]}s$. Finally choose a rational $\\sigma\\in(0,1)$ with $$\\label{eq:source-soundness}\n 4^r d^2\\sigma<\\varepsilon.$$ Only now invoke Theorem <a href=\"#thm:label-cover\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:label-cover\">3</a> for this fixed $\\sigma$. Its constant alphabets may be large, but no preceding choice depends on their sizes or on the input formula.\n\nAll objects used to run the reduction are finite integers or finite rational probability tables. For each fixed $\\delta$, choose them once and incorporate their finite descriptions into one algorithm. Neither a uniform runtime as $\\delta\\to0$ nor an algorithm computing all constants from $\\delta$ is asserted or needed. There is no input-dependent advice. The real tolerances, continuous functions, junta approximants, and alignments enter only the analysis.\n\n<details id=\"proof-stmt-prop-aligned-bound-2\"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-aligned-bound</summary>\n\n*Proof of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>.* It suffices to treat rational $0<\\delta<1/3$: for a fixed real threshold, choose a fixed rational $0<\\delta_0<\\delta$ and use the reduction for $\\delta_0$. Fix rational $\\delta$ and choose the constants as above, with $8\\varepsilon<\\delta$. Use the preprocessing and graph construction of Section <a href=\"#sec:construction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:construction\">3</a>.\n\nThe two preprocessing branches already have the required promises.",
